2026-10-17T07:19:53.2859130Z   QWEN_COMMIT_MESSAGE: [cluster:aspire2ap] [nodes:2] Raise ASPIRE P2/D2 concurrency to 104 after neither stage saturated at 88

Run 39010679169 (e18f3c6, P2/D2, c88) scored 4360.82 good output tok/s,
eligible at 98.6% good requests, up 12.0% from the P3/D1 record of
3895.05. ITL failures dropped to zero (p99 15.0 ms, decode 7.5 running
per rank), round_robin removed the prefill DP skew (685/675/682/704
seqs per rank versus 450/411/368/328 before), and every one of the 37
TTFT failures was a request issued at second 0.0 of the profiling
phase, the workload's start burst; steady-state TTFT p99 is 1.8 s.

The logs show the run was concurrency-limited rather than stage-limited:
prefill ranks had a queue in under 5% of seconds and processed about
40% more sequences per second in those seconds than their steady rate,
decode running plus in-transfer sat at 10-11 against a slot cap of 23,
and throughput equals 88 / 3.92 s of request latency. TTFT of 1.4 s is
mostly handoff latency (about 0.9 bootstrap waits per prefill rank plus
KV transfer), which costs no GPU time.

Change only the client concurrency, 88 to 104. If prefill capacity does
not grow at all, steady TTFT rises by 16/22 s to about 2.1 s average and
2.5 s p99, still inside the SLA, and start-burst failures grow to about
2%. If capacity is 26-28 req/s as the burst rates suggest, throughput
rises 18-27% with TTFT near 1.5 s. 112 was rejected because its
worst-case steady p99 approaches 2.9 s. Decode load at 104 is about 9
per rank, ITL about 13 ms. Pool, image, dispatch and topology are
unchanged from e18f3c6. Project balance before this run: 8772 SU with
the previous run's 1024 SU reservation still in doubt (actual use about
400 SU). Tests pass; results and reasoning are in the research note.
